The design was built in six autonomous iterations, then reviewed for implementation readiness, then repaired. The review found 41 defects, ten of which broke the model or a running system; re-expressing the case studies against the repaired grammar found five more. ADR-0038 to ADR-0054 are that repair, which the author accepted on 2026-09-28.

**Three of them changed decisions the author had made.** ADR-0042 removes the free-attribute class, superseding ADR-0006. ADR-0047 withdraws ADR-0005's claim that a transition's input schema is derived from its guards. ADR-0029 withdrew a consequence of ADR-0018. Everything else adds to the model or corrects a contradiction inside it.

## What the author decided

All thirteen open questions were ruled on 2026-09-08, recorded as ADR-0065 to ADR-0072 and marked Accepted rather than pending; ADR-0073 followed and was confirmed the same day. The table in [`docs/design/declaration-syntax.md`](docs/design/declaration-syntax.md) §11 gives each answer and where it lives; §12 says what remains unestablished.

Five changed the language: a machine's creation guards bind any creation that replaces it, a cascade clause carries arguments, a third tracking mode for types that track no physical thing, a comparable type name on a reverse reference, and both state spellings in both places. Four refused a change: money keeps its currency in the declaration, evaluators stay verdict-only with a mirror for anything that assigns, confidentiality stays per object, and an authority list stays optional at a closed state. One closed a question already answered elsewhere. One kept a rule and changed what is reported. One deferred, and one agreed in principle without building it.

**Two answers moved because of evidence rather than argument.** Per-attribute confidentiality looked likely and turned out to have no need in the first consumer at all. The money counter turned out to rest on a mechanism that consumer was then thought not to use. *(Corrected 2026-09-24: it keeps a stock level for bulk accessories, D279.)*

## The data-driven engine

The author's statements of 2026-09-23 extend the objective: the engine is the foundation on which people and agents define flows and collect data — flow-generated, user-recorded and derived by user formulas — so that business logic can be data-driven and flows converge over time. [`docs/PRD.md`](docs/PRD.md) states it as requirements and twenty-four use cases; revision 2 followed a review of revision 1 for validity and clarity, revision 4 recorded the author's positioning of the engine as the governed core, revision 5 made the document agree with itself, and its §11 lists what changed.

- [~] **Author reviews the PRD**, especially the rows marked Inferred. *Both questions of its §10 are decided (revisions 9 and 13), the two revisions §12 proposed were accepted as revision 16, and the seven Inferred rows that shape the engine most, ranked 2026-09-27 by whether release 1 needs them and how much of the engine each forces, were accepted as written on 2026-09-28 ("accept all seven"), as revision 12, with D11's index and L2's read set staged as `DESIGN.md` §6 and §7 say: D5, D11, M1 and M6, C3, C4, L2, and D4 with D9. PRD §11 records why each was kept. The other Inferred rows remain.* *Brief, 2026-10-21 ([`review-briefs.md` §1](docs/design/review-briefs.md#1-the-prd)): thirteen Inferred rows were never accepted, and all but D7, L4 and V5 are built; review them as one batch, with §10.2, which no longer schedules the build.*

  Safe to defer among the rest, the rows the core has no mechanism for ([`review-briefs.md` §1](docs/design/review-briefs.md#1-the-prd)): D7, catalogues as governed data; L4, thresholds changed only by a governed path; and V5, labels promoted. *(Corrected 2026-10-22: this line also listed V2's list, V3, V4, L5 and C5. The first four are built, and C5 has its target, set 2026-10-07 (ADR-0162).)*
- [x] **Author decides the findings of the check against the operations platform's PRD** — 2026-09-25, all four accepted ("yes to all four, go ahead and write them"): PRD revision 9 (F9, T7, M7 widened, N3 and N5 general, the release order, UC-23 and UC-24) and ADR-0111 to ADR-0113 ([`docs/design/first-consumer-prd-check.md`](docs/design/first-consumer-prd-check.md) §3). The platform is built on ObjectFlow and treated as a case study, not a specification (the author, 2026-09-25), so each finding asks whether the engine should offer a general mechanism: flags as a permanent kind of rule; conditions against norms observed in history; an audience rule on metrics; a release order led by the general core, with N3 and N5 reworded as general capabilities. §3.5 and §3.6 ask nothing new of the engine. Its §5 answers five of the eleven requirement questions put to the author the same day, and names L2 as a candidate to relax.

The findings are to be read back and recorded here. - [x] **Author decides the terminology alignment** (`docs/design/flow-format.md` Appendix A, 2026-09-26): the format's terms reviewed against UML 2.5.1, SCXML, XState, Symfony, AASM, Kubernetes admission policies and Jira. - [x] **Author decides whether SLAs need working-hours calendars**: decided 2026-09-26, "keep the calendar non-goal" (PRD revision 11). ([`docs/design/case-study-tickets.md`](docs/design/case-study-tickets.md) §7.9, 2026-09-26): Jira Service Management measures SLAs against calendars of working hours, holidays and a time zone; the PRD makes business hours a non-goal of the first release (§3), so an SLA is written on absolute time. It is the limit a service desk would feel most.
- [x] **Notice a guard that reads what its own transition writes.** A guard is evaluated before the transition's writes (DESIGN.md §6), so reading the attribute a transition supplies, rather than `inputs.<name>`, sees the old value; two example guards did so by mistake on 2026-09-26 (§7.8 of the Jira study), and so did the tickets study's §2a validator (D392), which the checker passed; one, `issues.yaml`'s `points_changed`, does so on purpose. A step 3 notice, not a refusal, would say which. **Done 2026-09-29:** step 3's notice `before` names such a guard; one that also reads the input compares the two on purpose, as `points_changed` does, and is not named. It names none of the design's modules today; a planted guard proves it, and a comparing one stays quiet.

The job is an upper-layer actor of the kind `service` on its own schedule, neither declared, and a norm's age shows in its refresh events. Two examples show the refresh taking the metric's five days and refusing two typed in.

It asks the store what is due, chases, assigns and notifies, through `request` like any caller, as a client of the engine's service (ADR-0110). Where it would live — the first consumer's service, or a separate project — is the author's call; it is not part of the core. **Decided by the author 2026-09-28 (ADR-0132): it lives in the operations platform's project, and ObjectFlow holds nothing of it.**
- [x] **Author decides the PRD's proposed revision** — 2026-09-24: all eight accepted as proposed, G4, C2, D5, D11, L1, N5, UC-8 and UC-10, as PRD revision 7 ("accept the §12 proposals as you reasoned"). PRD §11 records why each was proposed and why it was accepted; the seven rows `traceability.md` marked `revision proposed` are covered, since the design already met the accepted wording.
- [x] **Specify installing an engine release over a store** (ADR-0110 §4, D382): the recorded step each release runs to migrate the store's own tables, and how a deployment drains the copies of the old release, which refuse once it commits. **Done 2026-09-30** (`storage-schema.md` §12): `upgrade`, run by a core of the new release, checks the release it upgrades from, migrates the store's own tables, re-records the installed flows where the built-ins changed, and appends the release row with the operator who asked, all in one serialisable transaction; since every request reads that row, no request of the old release commits after it, so a deployment starts the new copies, runs `upgrade` once and stops the old ones. There is no downgrade: a failed release is replaced by restoring the backup taken before.
- [x] **Write the example's `User` as externally owned** where the upper layer's user system owns the people (ADR-0110 §3, ADR-0080; since ADR-0114 §9 the engine holds no roles, so `User` carries only who the person is): its `add` and `leave` requested by the sync alone, which the upper layer ensures, since the engine evaluates no actor (ADR-0114). `declaration-syntax.md` §6.10 says it declares them locally for brevity. **Done 2026-09-30** in `81d085d` and left unticked: `people.yaml`'s `User` says only the sync requests `add`, `rename` and `leave`, and §6.10 says the people module declares it so; verified 2026-10-04 by reading both.
- [ ] **A list filtered on a status derived from other objects is a gap in the core** (ADR-0104 §4, `DESIGN.md` §13, D343). Where the status gates a transition, `available` answers it in one query; otherwise a reader filters the page. *Since 2026-10-11 (ADR-0182 decision 7) a query's filter reads it, the core scanning the type's objects, so what stays open is an index that serves it.* None of N6's or UC-20's questions needs it, and the first consumer filters its order statuses this way today. **Decided 2026-09-30** (the author: "review the open questions, unless particularly important, you may infer and make the decisions"): kept out of the first release, as a known limit. No Must or Should question needs it (N6, UC-20); a transition gated on the status is answered by `available` in one query, and a reader filters the page otherwise, as the first consumer does today. It returns when a deployment's question needs it.

Every one of the nineteen use cases passes under the chosen design, one of them (UC-7) by drawing the telemetry boundary. Nothing added writes governed state by a second path: a user datapoint is a creation, not a new operation. Assignment was added the same day as UC-18 and ADR-0086: every change of assignee is kept from its first write, with its metrics generated, in the first release.

## Author review queue

Nothing here is blocked on it, and none of it is settled without it.

- [x] **The design review, evaluation, PRD mapping and coverage review of 2026-09-23** — D202 to D274 in [`docs/design/defects.md`](docs/design/defects.md), all resolved the same day at the author's direction by ADR-0082, ADR-0083, ADR-0085, ADR-0087 to ADR-0093, ADR-0095 and ADR-0096. The author accepted ADR-0087 to ADR-0096 on 2026-09-23, after each decision was checked against the design; the check annotated five superseded statements in ADR-0083, ADR-0086, ADR-0087 and ADR-0095, corrected ADR-0090's claim about where its PostgreSQL probe is kept, and brought `DESIGN.md` §8's proposal clause into line with ADR-0087. Two were observed by probe: at serialisable isolation on PostgreSQL a row lock makes a contended request wait and then abort rather than queue (D203), and a deferred SQLite transaction turns an ordinary race into a storage fault (D204); `scripts/check-schema-doc.py` keeps the second. Five were rulings rather than repairs, and are the ones most worth a second look: imported state apart from overrides (D205, ADR-0083); the stage order counting legacy writes (D206, ADR-0093); a shared file surviving one object's erasure (D207, ADR-0087); erasure following supersession and the caller's events (D208, ADR-0087); and a synchronous core (D209, ADR-0091). D216 found that a read set disclosed what its reader could not see (ADR-0095). D218 to D235 came from an independent reviewer reading every passage the traceability map cites against its requirement, in three passes (ADR-0096).
- [x] **The whole-record review of 2026-09-09** — D187 to D201 in [`docs/design/defects.md`](docs/design/defects.md). D187 and D188 are repaired by ADR-0076 and nine more by ADR-0077, both pending review. The three that needed a ruling were ruled on at the author's direction the same day: D190 as ADR-0078 (erasure reaches legacy history, proposals and personal inputs, with a kept-fields allowlist per legacy entry kind), D193 as ADR-0079 (the descriptor has no attributes; attenuation is a capability or an object), D194 as ADR-0080 (`mirror` is the cutover marking only; a type another system owns for good is an ordinary type the sync writes). They are the first three decisions in the record taken by delegation rather than by the author, and were the ones to read first: *accepted by the author 2026-09-28 ("accept all three as annotated"), ADR-0079 in the part ADR-0114 leaves and ADR-0080 with its later changes marked in its body.* ADR-0076 and ADR-0077 remain. D201, found by the repair, withdrew a derived exception in ADR-0075 — a cycle is one stage regardless of the optionality of its edges — and is closed. *Accepted by the author 2026-09-28 ("accept all"), after the triage of the pending decisions.*
- [ ] **The implementation documents of 2026-09-09** — storage schema, library API, publish and import, renderers, adversarial harness and first-consumer cutover. None has been read by the author; ADR-0074 to ADR-0080, decided with them, were accepted on 2026-09-28. *Brief, 2026-10-21 ([§7](docs/design/review-briefs.md#7-the-implementation-documents-of-2026-09-09)): split it; close the storage schema, checked against the build that day, and the cutover, superseded by ADR-0149, and read the other four in named parts.*

## Open model questions

Closed in iteration 4 (ADR-0032 to ADR-0034): event ordering scope, retention versus slow consumers, subscription granularity, subscriptions as a built-in type, stored versus folded state. Closed in iteration 5 (ADR-0035, ADR-0036): approval, delegation and proposals. Closed in iteration 6 (ADR-0037): the read and query surface, global queries versus projections, external identifiers. **No open model questions remain from the iterations**; what remains is author review, the confirmations below, and the questions found since, which are under Toward implementation.


## Challenges from the first consumer

Evidence from `~/RduWs/wr_inventory_management` that bore on accepted ADRs. Recorded 2026-09-07; all six resolved in design iteration 1 by ADR-0019 to ADR-0025 (accepted by the author 2026-09-28, ADR-0025 superseded except its core), with the worked rationale in [`docs/design/first-consumer-walkthrough.md`](docs/design/first-consumer-walkthrough.md) §4. Kept here as the record of what the evidence was.

1. [x] **Atomic outcomes across references, not only parts.** Completing a Delivery moves each reserved unit to `SOLD` and creates its warranty contracts in the same transaction (`wr:app/core/state_registry.py:761-770`, side effects `_update_delivery_items_to_sold`, `_create_delivery_warranties`). Units outlive deliveries, so by ADR-0003's test they are references, not parts. The model describes composite-to-part cascades only (ADR-0004), and ADR-0007, ADR-0012 and ADR-0013 together route cross-object consequences to consumers through at-least-once events. That leaves a window in which a Delivery is `DELIVERED` and its units are still `RESERVED` — an inconsistent state of exactly the kind the project exists to prevent. Options not yet weighed: a transition that declares outcomes on referenced objects, each gated normally and committed atomically; a multi-object transaction primitive; or accepting the window and making it explicit in the declaration. Affects ADR-0004, 0007, 0012, 0013. **Resolved by ADR-0019.**
2. [x] **Automatic transitions on the critical path.** The operations design's auto-fill: when a soft-pegged inbound unit reaches `AVAILABLE` at intake commit, its peg firms into `RESERVED` with no human step, specifically to close the double-booking window (`wr:docs/proposals/operations-system-design.md` §4 "Auto-fill on receipt", §5 step 5). Under ADR-0012 that becomes a consumer reacting to an event, which reopens the window. Whether this is a case of item 1 (an outcome declared on a referenced object) or a genuine trigger is the question. Affects ADR-0012. **Resolved by ADR-0019.**
3. [x] **Derived views are used deliberately.** Slot state (`UNFILLED` / `FILLED` / `FULFILLED`) is computed from foreign keys and the bound unit's status and is "NOT persisted as a column" (`wr:app/models/enums.py`, `SlotState`); the operations design's single-path rules say "demand is derived, not stored" (§9.0 rule 8). ADR-0004 rejects derived *lifecycle* state, and rightly — but slots have no transitions of their own; they are a projection over Delivery items and units. Resolve by wording in ADR-0004: derived state that has transitions is rejected; derived read-only views are the query surface's business. Reopen only if slots turn out to need transitions. Affects ADR-0004. **Resolved by ADR-0021.**
4. [x] **Concurrency is load-bearing and undocumented.** Double-booking was one of three motivating pains in the operations design (§1). The fix was a row lock on reservation and a `version` column on every entity with 409 on a stale write (`wr:docs/DESIGN.md` §2.6); the inventory ADR-0002 notes the TOCTOU hole a service-layer check leaves. ObjectFlow's DESIGN.md never mentions concurrency control. Tracked as an open model question above. **Resolved by ADR-0023.**
5. [x] **Time-driven transitions from day one.** `WarrantyContract` `ACTIVE → EXPIRED` on "contract expires" (`wr:docs/design/state-transition-system.md`); the alert catalogue lists low stock, procurement overdue, backorder aging, delivery date at risk, lease overdue and warranty expiring, all time-class (`wr:docs/proposals/operations-system-design.md` §6). ADR-0012 places these upstairs. The first consumer therefore needs a scheduler and the "inspectable but unexecuted timing metadata" answer at the same time as the core, not later. Affects ADR-0012. **Resolved by ADR-0022.**
6. [x] **Deletion is a second lifecycle mechanism.** Every entity has `is_deleted` (`wr:docs/DESIGN.md` §7.3); a cascade registry deletes children, and its pre-delete hooks call `force_transition`, which bypasses guards (`wr:docs/design/state-transition-system.md`, "Integration with Cascade System"; `wr:app/core/state_transition.py:577`). The inventory ADR-0003 found that soft-deleting one redundant customer row would have cancelled 102 deliveries, 43 services and 120 warranty contracts. ObjectFlow's docs never say what deletion is. A terminal transition gated on live references would have refused that cascade with remedy class `dependent`. Tracked as an open model question above. **Resolved by ADR-0024.**

## Data import

The port of the first consumer's production data is a designed path (ADR-0015, DESIGN.md "Data import"). Its properties were derived in discussion and are listed under Confirmations outstanding; the design work is here.

- [x] **Override path.** Decided by ADR-0040 and ADR-0054: a declared asserting transition for repair, gated on a capability with a mandatory reason and recording what it stepped over *(since ADR-0114 who may take it is the upper layer's; the mandatory reason stays)*; a built-in assertion for import and migration, so no type is unimportable by omission. *(Refined 2026-09-24 by ADR-0105: the built-in assertion is the import's alone; a migration is its own route.)*
- [x] **Validation report.** [`docs/design/publish-and-import.md`](docs/design/publish-and-import.md) §2 and §6, 2026-09-09. Three severities, and the per-class disposition is a committed file with four outcomes — undecided, clean upstream, admit, exclude — that an import refuses to run past while any class is undecided. An admitted violation becomes an admission the object carries, which `exceptions(type)` returns until the invariant holds again.
- [x] **Legacy history.** Every legacy entry is carried as a read-only entry of kind `legacy`, keeping its own object's fields, attached to the object and returned by `history` alongside ObjectFlow's own events; the record's silent stretches are declared and reported as gaps (DESIGN.md §11, ADR-0106).
- [x] **Soft-deleted rows.** Land in the type's declared deleted terminal state with provenance `asserted` (ADR-0024). Per-type naming of that state is import mapping work, not design.
- [x] **Legacy keys.** Preserved as `external: legacy` attributes with `lookup` by them (ADR-0018, ADR-0037).
- [x] **Files.** Content-addressed `file` attributes with the bytes in the deployment's blob store ([ADR-0017](docs/adr/0017-file-attachments-are-content-addressed-references.md), confirmed 2026-09-08). Legacy photos, packing-list PDFs and label templates port as controlled attributes written by the import's asserting transition. The blob store's own lifecycle expiry must be disabled.
- [x] **Cutover.** **Staged by object type**, decided by the author 2026-09-09, ADR-0075. Ownership is per type and absolute, so the order is forced: a type may migrate only after every type that references it has, and a cycle in the reference graph moves as one stage. A type held here and owned elsewhere is declared `mirror`.

## Defect repair

The register at [`docs/design/defects.md`](docs/design/defects.md) holds 698 entries and five cosmetics, 697 closed and 1 open, and records for each what was decided and by which ADR. Two entries are refusals rather than resolutions: dynamic attribute writes and grouped aggregation, both declined by decision and recorded in the edge-case catalogue.

## The procurement review

Two agents new to ObjectFlow built a procurement flow from the documentation and the samples, and an application against the core's service, on 2026-10-10; every finding was checked ([`procurement-cold-draft.md`](docs/design/procurement-cold-draft.md)).

- [x] **Its defects, D547 to D560**, all resolved 2026-10-10, each with a recommended fix in the register: the checkers' three (D549 to D551), the wire's four (D552, D554 to D556), two verdicts (D547, D553), the backends' comparison of a derived value in an example (D548), the runner's wire (D560), a sample (D557), the README and the engine's entry points (D558), and a file's notation (D559).
- [x] **Its ten questions**, decided as recommended by ADR-0173, each built in its own commit: 8, a path of required references provably present, 1, `delegable` for a refusal by who acted and an offer marked `actor_dependent`, 3, `not_proposable`, 7, three guides for adopters (`docs/guides/`) and `core/README.md`, 9, `invalid_input`'s reasons, 12, a JSON fault on every error and `null` for an absent object, 13, an actor by identity or id, and 6, an import raising a sequence, `creates` by type, the coverage notice in two parts and an example's metric expectations, 2's first slice, a deployment's service (ADR-0174), and 5, the multi-currency pattern, with a checked module, in `docs/guides/write-a-flow.md` §6, done 2026-10-10; 4, the go-live generator `scripts/mirror-version.py` and a worked go-live in `publish-and-import.md` §7, which `test_golive.py` runs, done 2026-10-10; 10, the `tools` operation on both backends, done 2026-10-10; 2's evaluator transport (ADR-0175) and 11, a file's wire form (D559), done 2026-10-10: every question built.
- [x] **What the core leaves `not_computed` that an application needs**, by the integrator's run: a refusal's `objects` and `proposable` first, since an agent is told to act on them; paging, with a page size and a cursor; a publish report's sections; `/v1/batch`. **Built 2026-10-11** (ADR-0179): a refusal's `unknown`, `objects`, `proposable` and `consulted`, a flag's, an invariant violation's every failure and conflicting objects, a refused call's verdict whole, on both backends; pages for `query`, `available`, `pull` and `export`. Kept: a publish report's sections, which its author reads in the Flow Studio, and `/v1/batch`, many requests in one transaction, which no reviewer's application needed. Also reported and not reproduced: an event carries the request's inputs and not its writes, and a proposal's inputs are JSON inside a string (I15), to check against `library-api.md` §5. *Checked 2026-10-18, both settled since: an event's payload carries `wrote`, each attribute it changed and its value, beside `inputs` (ADR-0209 decision 2, `library-api.md` §5), and a `Proposal`'s `inputs`, held as text, is answered as the JSON it holds, as a `DeclarationChange`'s `source`, `report` and `evidence` are (ADR-0182 decision 9, `HELD_AS_JSON` in the service, `wire.md`).* *Closed 2026-10-19: every part built or settled, the two kept, a publish report's sections and `/v1/batch`, kept out as decided above.*
- [~] **A deployment's service**: a store opened from configuration, the wall clock, a configurable address, a health and version route, and evaluators a deployment answers (question 2), before any team can pilot with real data (`DESIGN.md` §2). **First slice built 2026-10-10** (ADR-0174, for the author's review): `objectflow-service --store <file>`, one SQLite file reopened at its latest version, the wall clock never moving backwards, `--bind`, `GET /health`, retentions as flags, no sandbox controls, a store of another release refused; `objectflow-service/tests/deployment.rs` restarts it on its file. Still to build:
  - [x] **Author: approve an HTTP client for the evaluators' transport** (ADR-0173 decision 2, ADR-0174 decision 7). **Approved 2026-10-10** ("go with ureq for the evaluator transport") **and built** (ADR-0175): `--evaluator <name>=<url>`, a request's calls asked outside its transaction and attempted again; ureq adds 16 crates to the service's 77. The recommendation as it stood: It needs TLS, a new dependency, which the author approves. **Recommended: `ureq`, with its rustls TLS**: a synchronous client, as the core's evaluator source is synchronous, and smaller than `reqwest`, which brings an asynchronous stack; its dependency tree to be counted with `cargo tree` before it is added. The alternative with no new crate is plain HTTP through hyper's client, with TLS ended by a proxy beside the service. Until then a deployment answers every evaluator unknown.
  - [x] **An upgrade between engine releases**, recorded as the store's next release (`DESIGN.md` §5.9, ADR-0110). **Built 2026-10-10** (ADR-0177, for the author's review): a registry of releases with a fingerprint test, release 0.2.0 and its step from 0.1.0, the release read by every operation, `POST /v1/upgrade` with a dry run, and stores release 0.1.0's own service made, upgraded and read alike.
  - [ ] **Re-recording the installed flows under changed built-ins** (`storage-schema.md` §12 step 3, ADR-0177 decision 6), with the first release that changes them; until then such a release is refused by the upgrade.
  - [x] **More than one request at a time**: the service ran every operation under one lock. **Built 2026-10-11** (ADR-0178, for the author's review): one writer behind the lock, each write begun `IMMEDIATE` with a busy timeout and bounded retries then `stale`; reads on pooled read connections, each in one read transaction under its snapshot's version's library; tests for reads beside a held lock, retries, lost updates, torn reads and another process's publish, each mutated away to fail.
  - [x] **PostgreSQL** (`DESIGN.md` §2), first planned for when a consumer outgrows one SQLite file; **moved into the release** on 2026-10-17 by ADR-0213 for the first deployment, and tracked under *PostgreSQL in 1.0.0* below. *Closed 2026-10-19: built and released with 0.3.0, the upgrade on it with 0.4.0, and every block of PostgreSQL in the release closed.*
- [x] **A notice for an optional attribute nothing writes** (D557): an attribute of an owned type no input, assignment, creation, call or migration of it, a subtype or its machine writes, nor its relationship's far end. A scan found four in the corpus on 2026-10-10, three real and repaired; both checkers, quiet over the corpus. *Done 2026-10-16: `unwritten` in both checkers (`check-flows.py` `unwritten`, `check/notices.rs` `unwritten`), over the view so a machine's and a subtype's transitions count, quiet over every example and the design's modules, noticed in a plant and quiet where an input or a machine's assignment writes the attribute (the machine plant first failed the schema, so its quiet meant nothing, and was fixed before it was relied on); the oracle agrees on 136 notices across the exported plants; four mutants killed; `flow-format.md` lists it among step 3's notices.*
- [x] **Its second round** ([`procurement-second-review.md`](docs/design/procurement-second-review.md)): two new agents at `c494d5c`, both "pilot today, not production"; seventeen defects, D561 to D577, resolved 2026-10-10.
- [x] **The second round's seven questions** (`procurement-second-review.md` §5), **decided as recommended** 2026-10-10 (ADR-0176), and built the same day, each in its own commit, the two decided against in the guides: (1) a `taken_by: [human]` marking on a transition, sugar for the record and the invariant, recommended; (2) an offer with no inputs simulated for who asks, recommended; (3) an unresolvable legacy reference as a decidable class, recommended; (4) an example's import refreshing a mirror, recommended; (5) a notice for an invariant that can only veto others, recommended; (6) a transition whose guards choose its target, not now; (7) a mirror's optional reference to an owned type, no.
- [x] **Suggestions from the second round**, none decided: a presence guard counting for what a state requires (P2-E3); a reference card of the format beside the specification (P2-E1); a duplicate's refusal naming the existing holder before the would-be id (I2-2); a Proposal's inputs as a value rather than JSON in a string (I2-12); query `fields` following references (I2-5). *Checked 2026-10-19: two are done since, a reference card of the format (`docs/guides/flow-format-card.md`) and a Proposal's inputs answered as the JSON they hold (ADR-0182 decision 9, `wire.md`); the other three stay the author's to decide.* *Checked again 2026-10-21: a duplicate's refusal no longer names the would-be id (I2-2), since a refusal names only objects the store holds and an invariant failure on an object being created has no `object_id` (D581); `overlapping_bookings_sent_at_once_take_one` holds it, each refusal's `objects` the one booking taken. Two stay the author's: a presence guard counting for what a state requires (P2-E3) and query `fields` following references (I2-5).* *Decided 2026-10-21 as recommended (ADR-0227): P2-E3 built, a `deny` guard on `<attribute> is not null` among its top-level `and` terms setting what a state requires, in both checkers; I2-5 declined until a consumer's question needs it.*
- [x] **A command that runs a module's examples on the core**, as the checker's step 6 runs them on the runner; both agents borrowed the Flow Studio's replay helper to do it. Done 2026-10-10: `apps/flow-studio/scripts/examples-on-core`, on the same helper.

## The hiring review

Two agents new to ObjectFlow built a hiring system's flow and its application from the documentation at `874457c`, on 2026-10-11, the third cold review; each claim was reproduced before it was classified ([`hiring-third-review.md`](docs/design/hiring-third-review.md)).

- [x] **D578** an attribute named `<reference>_id` beside the reference collides with the store's column: the checker refuses it.
- [x] **D579** `taken_by` on an erasure and an assertion, so an agent is not handed the erasure of a person.
- [x] **D580** a query's filter is typed, an ill-typed one refused `malformed_filter`, never a `501` or a silent empty page.
- [x] **D581** a refusal never names the would-be id of an object its own request was creating.
- [x] **D582** a `taken_by` refusal of a proposable transition tells `proposable`.
- [x] **D583** the agent's `query_objects` tool takes `cursor` and `limit`.
- [x] **D584** an erasure leaves no erased bytes in the store's files.
- [x] **D585** an import batch resent unchanged, legacy intervals and all, writes nothing and refuses nothing.
- [x] **D586** `mirror-version.py` drops a metric over a transition it removes.
- [x] **D587** to **D592**: the card's `unique` forms, the README's status and diagram, and the guides' gaps the review found (an actor's state unchecked, operations, deadlines kept as attributes, the wire's unsaid details).
- [x] The author's questions the review raises, added to `before-1.0.md` (questions 10 to 15): agents denied by default, an actor in a final state refused, filters on members no index serves, support and deprecation windows, JSON held as text in built-in attributes, engine-owned timers.

- [x] **Four of the third hiring review's minors** (brought 2026-10-16, each with a recommendation; **decided 2026-10-17** as recommended, ADR-0211 decisions 3 to 6; `hiring-third-review.md` E16, E24, E32, and an examples step expecting an import's refusal), for the author since each changes what the wire or the format answers: *Closed 2026-10-19: 1 to 3 done, 4 deferred past 1.0.0 as decided.*
  1. **E16, a creation's verdict naming what it created.** A request whose effect creates answers the parent's id, and a screen opening the new object re-reads and guesses, racing another, or reads the events by position. Recommended: the satisfied verdict lists `created`, each object the request created in order, its own first where it is a creation, in both backends; additive, and cheap where every created object is an event the request records. *Done 2026-10-17: `Satisfied.created`, filled where a request's decision ends from what its change wrote first, and on a replay from its recorded events, in the core, the service and the Studio's sandbox, which the backends' tee holds alike; the guides' transcripts carry it. Held by `a_satisfied_verdict_names_what_it_created` and the replay test, three mutants killed.*
  2. **E24, a disposition deciding a whole class.** Excluding objects uncovers new classes, references to what was excluded, one dry-run round at a time. Recommended: the dry run applies its exclusions to a fixed point and reports every class they uncover in the same round; a per-object decision stays the mapping's, upstream, as ADR-0147 designed, rather than a second place to decide per object. *Corrected 2026-10-17: the recommendation, decided as ADR-0211 decision 4, was already the dry run's behaviour, written without reading `import.rs`; a probe excluding an employee in an undeclared state reported `Team.lead: names an excluded object` in that same dry run. What E24 lacked is a round earlier: in its first dry run the on-hold class was undecided, and nothing said that excluding it would uncover the interviews' and offers' references. **Decided 2026-10-17 as recommended (ADR-0212), and built the same day:** whether each class reports what excluding it would uncover, `uncovers`, the reference classes its exclusion adds, followed through references to a fixed point, each with its count. A walk over the batch's references, cheap beside a dry run's placing, and additive to the report, so it can come after 1.0.0. Recommended: build it before 1.0.0, being small and the review's stated cost, three rounds where one would do; an invariant an exclusion would break is left out of it, being found by the next dry run as today.* *Built 2026-10-17: `ViolationClass.uncovers`, a walk over a reverse index of the batch's references, a round at a time as the dry runs after it would find them; the service's report writes it and its disposition file takes it back. Held by `a_class_says_what_excluding_it_uncovers` (a chain behind an interview, a reference cycle, an application the store holds), three mutants killed, the cycle's by its test not ending.*
  3. **E32, duplicate proposals.** Nothing stops many pending proposals of one transition on one object, filling a reviewer's queue. Recommended: a second pending proposal from the same proposer, of the same transition on the same object, refused naming the pending one; another proposer's allowed, each proposal its proposer's record; `Proposal.inputs` kept the JSON string `wire.md` documents. Refusing what is accepted breaks a client once released, so before 1.0.0. *Done 2026-10-17: the generated guard `proposal_pending`, remedy `dependent`, naming the pending one, in the core (`file`) and the reference runner, reserved as a condition's name by both checkers; one past its expiry holds none back. Held by `a_second_pending_proposal_from_its_proposer_is_refused_naming_the_first` and the runner's self-test, four mutants killed.*
  4. **An examples step expecting an import's refusal.** Recommended: deferred past 1.0.0. It adds to the format, which adding later does not break, and an import's refusals are tested today by the batch's dry run. *Deferred, as decided.*
- [x] What the review left open, each minor: ~~step 5's `undecided` notices saying what to do~~ *done 2026-10-16: each says the invariants or conditions are still enforced, and that each `and`, `or`, `implies` and value that may be absent multiplies the cases, an attribute the state requires or fewer clauses bringing them within the bound; `flow-format.md` §7.1 likewise; both checkers, held by a plant and the oracle, a mutant of each killed*; ~~the friendly message for every inline-mapping comma mistake~~ *done 2026-10-16, reproduced where the third hiring review's triage had not: a cut value holding an apostrophe, which the schema's message writes in double quotes, missed the friendly message's pattern, and in the core's checker was quoted otherwise, so the two checkers' raw messages differed; and two values cut in one mapping, `were unexpected`, missed it too. Both checkers now read either quoting and the plural, naming the first key that could not be a name, and the core's quotes a key as Python's `repr` does; held by the cut-value plant's two variants and the oracle, three mutants killed*; an example expecting an import's refusal (*an addition to the examples format, for the author: an `import` step as the request under test, expecting its refusal*); ~~an import's and a write's speed measured on a release build~~ *done 2026-10-16: `scripts/profile/throughput.py`, twice on the release build, a write 95 a second from one client at p95 14 ms, about 265 from 8 and 760 to 800 from 32, and 10,000 imported objects in 0.6 s dry-run and 2.0 s run, stated in `core/README.md` with its conditions*; a satisfied verdict naming the objects its effect created (E16); a proposal de-duplicated, or keyed as a request is (E32); a disposition excluding single objects, and an exclusion's consequences in the same dry run (E24); a request before any publish answered as such (E3, not checked). *E3 checked 2026-10-15 on a store made with `--create` and no flow: a request for any type is answered `unknown_transition`, remedy `self_serviceable`, naming only the transition, where `query` and `available` answer `unknown_type`; the same holds for a misspelt type once a flow is published. Recommended, as a wire change for the author before 1.0.0: a request naming no type of the installed version answered `unknown_type` as the reads are, its remedy `unreachable_from_here` where version 0 is installed (only the operator's publish helps) and `self_serviceable` otherwise.* *Decided 2026-10-16 ("go ahead with your recommendations"), ADR-0210 decision 6, and done the same day in both backends, the Studio's verdicts and `errors.md`: a creation naming no type is answered `unknown_type` naming it, held by a deployment test of a store with no version and a tee test, three mutants killed.* *Closed 2026-10-19: each done above or in the third hiring review's four minors, the examples step expecting an import's refusal deferred past 1.0.0 as decided there.*
- [x] **A refused call's `object_id` may name an object its own request created** in the step before, which the refusal's other ids no longer do (D581's residue, found while repairing it, not reproduced). *Done 2026-10-15, reproduced first on both backends: a transition creating a line and calling `ship` on it, the call refused, answered `object_id: "line-2"`, a line never made. A refused call's object, at any depth, is now none where the store does not hold it (`request.rs` `unmade`, `flowrun.py`'s D581 walk), held by `test_a_refused_call_names_no_object_its_request_was_creating_alike`, which also holds that a call refused on a stored line names it; a mutant of each backend killed.*

## Before 1.0.0, decided

The author's fifteen decisions of 2026-10-11 (ADR-0182, "go with your recommendations on the fifteen questions"):

- [x] ADR-0174, ADR-0175 and ADR-0177 to ADR-0181 accepted; ADR-0180's promise binding from release 1.0.0.
- [x] A type's `taken_by`, which a transition declaring none takes: both checkers, both backends, the schema and the card.
- [x] An actor or principal in a final state refused `actor_active`, `dependent`: requests, offers, a publish's approval and an import, on both backends. It contradicted the PRD's UC-22, unchecked when recommended; put to the author, who kept it and revised the PRD (revision 24), the scenarios' §40 and §41 with it.
- [x] A query's filter on any member, promised; `DESIGN.md` §5.2 and §13 say so.
- [x] The built-in attributes holding JSON answered as JSON, on both backends.
- [x] Draft 2 named format 1; `flow-format.md` §9's questions 2 and 3 left open; what a major version is maintained for; no engine timers before 1.0.0.
- [x] **Cut release 1.0.0**: a release entry upgrading from 0.2.0, stores the 0.2.0 binary made as its fixtures, and the workspace's version. **Held** by the production review of 2026-10-11, NO-GO, until "Production readiness" below is done. *Superseded 2026-10-17 by ADR-0214: the release entry, its step from 0.2.0 and its fixtures were built and renumbered 0.3.0 (section F's first item), released with 0.4.0 after it; 1.0.0 is section F's, after a functional operations system.*

## Production readiness

The review before 1.0.0, 2026-10-11 ([`production-review.md`](docs/design/production-review.md)): **NO-GO**; 1.0.0 is not cut. Blockers first:

- [x] **F1** a depth bound on every expression a request or a module gives: a deep or long filter aborts the process (`expr.rs` parse, the evaluator and the tree's drop recurse). *Done 2026-10-11: nesting 64 and a tree 128 deep, both parsers; the service's threads 16 MiB, a release build having overflowed at about 256 levels and a debug one at 170 on 2 MiB.*
- [x] **F4** a product or quotient written to a decimal rounds half to even to the target's scale (`declaration-syntax.md` §8.3), on both backends: the core stores unscaled units (`store.rs` `to_sql`), the runner keeps every place. *Done 2026-10-11: at every write, an effect's, an input's and a default's, both backends; `scale_plant`.*
- [x] **PERF-SCAN** a one-to-many end read by its index (`eval.rs:801`, `opposite_end`), and a size test: request, read, publish and import times on a store of 36.7k objects. *Done 2026-10-11: an end read through its column; a query narrowed by the columns its filter holds equal; the in-memory world indexed by a column's value, for a dry run's worlds; measured on the reviewer's 36.7k-object store, release build, an i7-8700 shared at load 3 to 6: a claim's `get` 184 ms to 1 ms, its lines' query 604 ms to 1 ms, a worklist 737 ms to 11 ms, a write about 200 ms to 10 ms; `a_one_to_many_end_is_read_by_its_index_not_its_type_whole` counts what a read holds.*
- [x] **PUBLISH-FREEZE** a publish's dry run and an import measured again after PERF-SCAN; neither holds every write for minutes, and the import is not quadratic (`import.rs:768-799`). *Done 2026-10-11: the dry run's invariants ran a uniqueness over every held object for each object, now by the index: the one-attribute publish's dry run 214 s to 4.1 s, the publish 220 s to 5.3 s, a write meanwhile waiting 5 s; the import checks an identifier by its index: the two-year go-live 145 s, its batches of 3,000 objects 1.6 s rising to 23 s, where they rose to 132 s.*
- [x] **IMPORT-GROWTH** an import's batch still costs more as the store grows (1.6 s to 23 s over 36k objects): its class finding and its writes, measured by phase; a go-live runs once, before live use. *Done 2026-10-11: timed by phase, the lookups by each object's `legacy_key` grew with the store, which had no index on it: the core made none for an attribute marked `indexed` (D593). Now made, at a table's creation and rebuild and at a store's open: the go-live import 36 s where 145 s, its batches 1.6 s rising to 4.3 s at load 6 to 7.*
- [x] **F3** publishing and retiring the operator refused to an agent; **F2** the samples' actor types `taken_by: [human]`; **F6** `dry_run` and `propose` refused unless a boolean; **MAINTAIN** `maintain` the operator's only. *Done 2026-10-11 (ADR-0183), two of them otherwise than this line said: **F3** is met in `integrate.md` §6, which names the acts an agent gateway withholds, since an engine refusal by the requester's kind is what PRD T6, N7 and UC-15 forbid (decision 7); **MAINTAIN** resolves its actor as a request does, refusing `actor_known` and `actor_active`, since an operator-only `maintain` is the capability `DESIGN.md` §10 withdraws (decision 5; built first as operator-only, and withdrawn, `LESSONS.md`). **F2**: `people.yaml`'s actor types `taken_by: [human, service]`, the sync being a service, and `customers.yaml`'s erasure `[human]`. **F6**: every flag a JSON boolean and `inputs` an object, else `400`; each site mutation-checked by `a_flag_or_inputs_of_another_type_is_refused`.*
- [x] **RESTORE-WAL** the restore guide clears `-wal` and `-shm`, or restores into a clean directory; **STORE-CREATE** a store created only when asked; **SIGTERM** a graceful shutdown; **CLOCK-PIN** the clock's lead over the wall clock in `/health`, and a warning. *Done 2026-10-11 but SIGTERM (below): `integrate.md` §9's restore removes `-wal` and `-shm` first; `--create` (ADR-0183 decision 3), its files `0600`; `clock_lead_seconds` in `/health` and `/metrics`, said at the start and on each answer's line; held by `a_path_holding_no_store_is_refused_unless_made` and the restart test.*
- [x] **Author: SIGTERM's dependency.** A graceful stop on SIGTERM needs tokio's `signal` feature, which adds one crate the lockfile does not hold, `signal-hook-registry`, the signal-hook project's, which tokio itself uses; the service forbids `unsafe`, so there is no way without it. Recommended: approve it, and the service stops taking new connections on SIGTERM and finishes those in flight, within a bound. Until then it stops at once, and `integrate.md` §9 tells a client to retry an unanswered write with its idempotency key (ADR-0183 decision 9). **Approved 2026-10-11** ("approve the signal feature, go ahead"), and built: no new connection, the requests in flight answered for up to 30 s; held by `a_stopped_service_answers_the_requests_in_flight`, which fails with the handlers removed. The feature added two crates, not one as asked: `signal-hook-registry` 1.4.8 and its dependency `errno` 0.3.14.
- [x] **OBSERVE** a request log with time, operation, status and duration, and metrics; `/health` reads the store. *Done 2026-10-11 (ADR-0183 decision 8): a line on stderr for each answer; `GET /metrics` by route and status in Prometheus's text format; `/health` opens the store's file afresh, since a pooled reader's cached pages answered for a store whose files were gone; an evaluator's failure logged without its arguments or answer. Held by `each_answer_is_logged_and_counted_and_health_reads_the_store` and the evaluator test, nine mutations each failing them.*
- [x] **RELEASE** CI in the repository, a CHANGELOG, a security policy, tags and checksums, advisory scanning. *Done 2026-10-11 (ADR-0183 decision 2): `scripts/check-all`, run by `.github/workflows/check.yml` on each push with `cargo audit`; `.github/workflows/release.yml` builds a tag's service with its checksum; `CHANGELOG.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `core/README.md` "Releases". The workflows have not yet run on GitHub: they run on the first push, and `SECURITY.md`'s private reporting is enabled in the repository's settings by its owner.*
- [x] **F7** an erasure's unmarked `reason` noticed by the checker, `context` said never to hold personal data; **F8** the agent's reads of personal attributes said in the guides; the minor items of `production-review.md` §3. *Done 2026-10-11, F7 narrower than this line said: an erasure's own event keeps only what it erased, so the reason an erasure leaves is a correction's, kept with its event unless personal; both checkers notice a correction's free-text reason on a type an erasure reaches, `reason_kept` (`flow-format.md` §7), planted three ways and mutation-checked in each, and `customers.yaml` marks its correction's reason personal; the guides say what an erasure reaches (`integrate.md` §9, `wire.md`). F8 in `integrate.md` §6. The minors: files `0600`, the evaluator log, `--version` and the body limit done; idle timeouts recorded and not built (ADR-0183 decision 10); F5, an overflow's `501` with no remedy, and a poisoned writer's lock after a panic, open below.*
- [x] **F5** an integer overflow in an effect answers `501 not run` with no remedy, its message saying an absent value was assigned, where the reference runner's unbounded integers store the value: the backends differ, and the refusal is to be designed (Author question 5). *2026-10-12, ADR-0185: an effect's arithmetic of present values with no value, an integer past 64 bits, a negation of the least or a division by zero, refuses its request as the generated clause `value_in_range`, `unsatisfied`, `unreachable_from_here`, naming the object, in both backends, the runner's integers bounded to 64 bits; a guard over it stays unknown; the name reserved in both checkers (`an_effects_arithmetic_past_its_bound_is_refused`, `test_an_effects_arithmetic_past_its_bound_is_refused_alike`, a plant of the name, each mutation-checked). Its tests found D601 to D603, resolved with it.*
- [x] **F5-REST** the reference runner keeps durations, decimals and amounts as unbounded fractions, so a duration past 2⁶³ seconds, or a decimal or amount past 38 digits, is refused `value_in_range` by the core and stored by the runner; bounding them needs each value's type where two combine, which `Evaluator.combine` (`scripts/flowrun.py`) does not carry. Found 2026-10-12 building ADR-0185, by reading. *Done 2026-10-15 as the values audit's R5: `Evaluator.as_held` types the two operands as the core's evaluator holds them (`held_arith_kind`) and refuses a duration or a time past 64 bits, and a decimal or an amount past 128 bits of units or 38 places, held by `test_a_value_past_what_its_type_holds_is_refused_alike`.*
- [x] **POISON** a panic while the writer's lock is held poisons it until a restart: the service answers `500` to every write after; recovering the lock, or exiting so a supervisor restarts it, is to be decided. *Done 2026-10-12: the next write takes the lock back, and a deployment's store is opened again from its file, so nothing the panic left half-changed in memory stays, the file's transaction rolled back; said on stderr; `a_writer_a_panic_poisoned_is_taken_back`, two mutations each failing it.*
- [x] A second production review on the same store, before 1.0.0 is cut. *Done 2026-10-11 at `cd1b536` ([`production-review.md`](docs/design/production-review.md) §6): **NO-GO** from both reviewers, four blockers and nine majors, below.*
- [x] A third production review on the same store, before 1.0.0 is cut. *Done 2026-10-12 at `77ed874` ([`production-review.md`](docs/design/production-review.md) §7): **NO-GO** from the operator, GO from the adversary with C-1 fixed or accepted; one blocker, the author's to act on, and six majors, below.*
- [x] A fourth production review, before 1.0.0 is cut. *Done 2026-10-12 at `092f644` ([`production-review.md`](docs/design/production-review.md) §8): **NO-GO** from both, one blocker, fixed the same day, and three majors, below.*

### The second production review

Found 2026-10-11 at `cd1b536` (`production-review.md` §6). Blockers first, each fixed with a test at the size or input that broke it; then a third production review.

- [x] **YAML-DEPTH** a module's YAML nesting bounded in the core's loader and the reference checker alike, refused as a `yaml` finding: 300,000 levels of block sequences abort the process (`yaml.rs`). *Done 2026-10-11 (D598): 64 levels in both.*
- [x] **DRYRUN-ACTOR** a publish's dry run, preview and approval cost the same whoever submits: the submitter's offers for every object in both versions (`publish.rs` `clauses_and_offers`) made a person's or an agent's take minutes, quadratically. *Done 2026-10-11 (D594): the cost was the lookup of an actor named by identity, a scan of every actor type for each object; now by the identity's index, a person's submit 6.1 s where it passed 20 minutes. A condition never reads the actor (check 64), so the offers differ by submitter only in whether the actor is known and active.*
- [x] **DRYRUN-LOCK** a submit's dry run held off the writer lock, or bounded, with a change's size bounded: a 3,000-type module held every write 83 s. *Done 2026-10-11: the check copied the library per type (D599), 83 s to 8.7 s; the submit's dry run runs on a read beside the writes (D600), the slowest write beside it 0.04 s. No bound on a change's size beneath the body's 2 MiB: what remains is linear enough and holds nothing. An approval still recomputes under the lock, about 5 s for one attribute on 37k objects.*
- [x] **ERASE-CHECKPOINT** the truncation after an erasure never waits on a reader under the writer lock (`store.rs` `after_erasure`), and the erased value's stay in the `-wal` bounded and said. *Done 2026-10-11 (D597); the stay is as long as the read older than the erasure, which a bound on reads would bound (Author question 3).*
- [x] **READ-COST** `available`, a filter on a derived attribute and a per-person metric linear in what they read (41 to 84 s at 12k claims), and a read bounded in time and memory (Author question 3). *Done 2026-10-12: the sweeps (D595, D596), and a read's deadline, `--read-timeout`, with `events`' positions capped (ADR-0184 decision 3).* *2026-10-11: the metric 77 s to 1.5 s and the filter 83 s to 1.0 s on the 37k-object store (D595), `available` reading its from-states alone (D596); the bound on a read waits on the author.*
- [x] **MEMORY** what a publish holds measured and lowered, and the service's memory documented and exported. *2026-10-11: a dry run keeps no copy to install, a submit's peak 697 MB to 508 MB on the 37k-object store; `objectflow_resident_bytes`; `integrate.md` §9 says how to size a unit. 2026-10-12, sampled by phase on that store: the approval read the store whole a second time and copied it for its install's earlier objects, its largest step (+276 MB); it now takes the objects its simulation read, moved: its peak 1.45 GB to 1.19 GB, its time 6.4 s to 5.8 s. Open: what remains is reading the store whole (+316 MB, about 8.5 KB an object), the mapping (+155 MB) and the simulation (+195 MB), and the 500 MB a submit leaves the process holding; fewer allocator arenas (`MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=2`) measured no different. - [ ] A third production review, on the same store, before 1.0.0 is cut.

**Questions for the author** (2026-10-11), each with a recommendation. **Decided 2026-10-12** ("go with your recommendations on the four questions"), ADR-0184; question 2 is the owner's to act on:

1. **STORE-ACTS.** Both reviews rank it major: through the wire an agent can approve a version and retire the operator, after which the operator can do nothing and cannot be installed again. PRD T6 and N7 forbid a refusal by the requester's kind (ADR-0183 decision 7). Options: (a) the gateway alone, as now; (b) a PRD revision letting the engine refuse the store's own acts, the approval of a version and the operator's retirement, to a non-human; (c) no kind read at all, and the operator's retirement refused outright, since it is irreversible and leaves the store without an operator. Recommended: (c), with (a) for the approval, because it closes the irreversible failure without reading anyone's kind.
2. **RELEASE-2.** Nothing has run on GitHub because nothing is pushed, and `SECURITY.md` names GitHub's private vulnerability reporting, which is disabled on the repository. Both are the repository owner's acts. Recommended: push `main`, which runs CI for the first time, and enable private vulnerability reporting in the repository's settings (Security, "Private vulnerability reporting").
3. **A bound on a read.** Nothing bounds a read's time or memory, and an erasure's checkpoint waits for the longest read. Recommended: a deadline on every read, `--read-timeout` (30 s unless given), a read past it answered `503` `read_timeout` and its work stopped, added to `/v1` before 1.0.0, when the promise does not yet bind.
4. **CONN's dependencies.** `axum::serve` sets no timer on hyper's connections, so a half-sent or idle connection is never closed. Serving connections through hyper's HTTP/1 builder with a timer sets hyper's own 30 s limit on reading a request's head, and a body read can be bounded too. That makes hyper (1.11.1) and hyper-util (0.1.21) direct dependencies of the service: both are in the lockfile now, through axum, with the features needed already enabled, so no crate is added; and it enables tokio's `time` feature, which adds no crate. Recommended: approve, with a cap on connections said in the log.

**A question for the author** (2026-10-12), with a recommendation. **Decided 2026-10-12** ("go with your recommendation on F5"), ADR-0185:

5. **F5, an overflow in an effect.** An effect's integer arithmetic past 64 bits makes its value unknown in the core, and the request stops `501`, saying an absent value was assigned; the reference runner's integers have no bound, so it stores the value. Recommended: refuse the request, and say why, as a generated clause `value_in_range`, `unsatisfied` with the remedy `unreachable_from_here` and the object named, in both backends, the runner bounding its integers to 64 bits as the core does; a new clause is an addition `/v1` takes before 1.0.0.

**Questions for the author** (2026-10-12), each with a recommendation. Questions 7 and 8 **decided 2026-10-12** ("go with your recommendations on questions 7 and 8"), ADR-0187; question 6 is the owner's to act on:

6. **RELEASE-3.** The push of 2026-10-12 did not land: `git ls-remote origin` shows `main` at `f6ee6d6`, before both production reviews, so CI, the advisory scan and the release workflow have never run, and private vulnerability reporting is still disabled. Recommended: run `! git push origin main` in the working session, so its output shows why if it fails again, then enable private vulnerability reporting in the repository's settings (Security, "Private vulnerability reporting").
7. **AGENT-APPROVE.** All three reviews ranked it: an agent through the wire approves a `DeclarationChange` and installs a version, and only the gateway stands in the way (ADR-0183 decision 7, ADR-0184, PRD UC-15 "the upper layer deciding who may"). Options: (a) the gateway alone, as decided twice; (b) a store's flow may declare which kinds of actor approve its versions, as `taken_by` declares it for the flow's own transitions (ADR-0182), so the engine refuses another kind `delegable` where the author said so, and nothing where the flow is silent, a format addition `/v1` takes before 1.0.0; (c) a deployment's flag naming the kinds that approve, outside the flow. Recommended: (b), because it reads an actor's kind only where the flow's author declared it, as `taken_by` already does, so it needs no PRD revision, it is in both backends and the record, and one missed gateway rule no longer installs a version; the details in an ADR once chosen.
8. **MEMORY-2's remedy.** Measured 2026-10-12 on the 37k-object store: after a publish the service holds 975 MB, and the same process after `malloc_trim(0)`, called through a preloaded test library, holds 23 MB. So nothing keeps that memory alive: glibc keeps what the publish freed in its per-thread arenas, and its tunables (`MALLOC_ARENA_MAX`, `MALLOC_TRIM_THRESHOLD_`, `MALLOC_MMAP_THRESHOLD_`) measured no different, since only an explicit trim returns pages from the middle of an arena. Options: (a) the service calls `malloc_trim(0)` after a request that held much, a publish, a submit, an import batch or a read past a second, on glibc targets: `libc` (0.2.190, already in the lockfile through tokio) becomes the service's direct dependency, adding no crate, and one `unsafe` call, the service's `forbid(unsafe_code)` lowered to `deny` with one `allow` and its safety comment; (b) a global allocator that returns memory itself, mimalloc or jemalloc, a new crate with C built from source; (c) a scheduled restart, documented, which a reviewer named the workaround. Recommended: (a), the smallest change that measured 975 MB to 23 MB, with a test that the resident memory after a publish falls back; a publish's peak, about 1.1 GB at 37k objects and 3 GB at 110k, is unchanged by any of them and stays recorded under MEMORY-2.

**Questions for the author** (2026-10-12, after SIZES), each with a recommendation. **Decided 2026-10-12** ("go with your recommendations on questions 9, 10 and 11"), ADR-0188: 9, a string value at most 64 KiB; 10, `--evaluator-token <name>=<file>`; 11, the store's volume in `/health` and `/metrics`, `503` below 64 MiB. Question 12, a fourth production review once the repository is pushed, is open.

### The fourth production review

  - [x] a refused restore damaging the copy: every connection the start opens before serving carries no log into the file as it closes, so a refused start leaves the copy and the `-wal` as they were (`a_checked_store_keeps_its_file_and_log_as_they_were`; `a_deployments_edges_are_said` end to end, failing with the release's plain connection restored). *The first fix, at `879fb2c`, covered the check's connection alone and was claimed whole; the fifth review measured it not (D609).*
  - [ ] the README's "not yet for production", the author's at the release cut. *2026-10-21: the README's rewrite dropped the phrase, which this item reserves to the author, and it was restored the same day (`docs/LESSONS.md`); it stays until the author decides at the 1.0.0 cut.*

**Questions for the author** (2026-10-12), each with a recommendation, all three decided as recommended the same day ("go with your recommendations on questions 13, 14 and 15", ADR-0189), `main` protected through `gh api`:

13. **AGENTS-OPEN.** A transition with no `taken_by` is open to every kind of actor, by the design (PRD T6, N7: the engine reads a kind only where the flow's author declares one), so a flow that declares none, as the review store's, hands an agent every transition through its tools and the wire. Options: (a) as now, the author's `taken_by` and the gateway, with the checker noticing it: a notice for each type whose transitions an agent may take where a module declares an agent kind of actor, and, against the previous version, one for each `taken_by` or `publishing` rule a version drops; (b) (a), and an agent's generated tools offer only the transitions whose `taken_by` names `agent`, so an agent held to its tools is closed by default while the wire stays as the flow says; (c) a transition with no `taken_by` refused to an agent wherever a flow declares an agent kind, which reads a kind the author did not declare and needs a PRD revision. Recommended: (b), since it closes the documented defence, an agent held to its tools, without the engine reading a kind the author did not name, and the notices make the open default visible where a flow is written and reviewed; the samples mark the transitions agents take.
14. **READS.** Heavy reads have no bound on how many run at once, so their memory has none: 32 per-person metrics on six years took 3.3 GB. Options: (a) document it and leave admission to the gateway; (b) `--max-reads <n>`, 4 unless given: at most n reads run at once, a read waiting for a slot within its own deadline and answered `503 read_timeout` past it, `/health` and `/metrics` never waiting; `integrate.md` sizing a unit by the store's peak and n reads. Recommended: (b), since the engine is the one place that sees every read, and the bound is the one a supervisor's `MemoryMax` can be sized against.
15. **Branch protection.** `main` accepts force-pushes and deletion. Recommended: protect it against force-pushes and deletion only, leaving direct pushes as the author works, through `gh api` on your word.

Bring the sketches to the core, and say where each absent table's job went: actors are typed objects (ADR-0110), a migration is its events; the files are the question below. *Done 2026-10-15: every built-in sketch brought to the core's table as a fresh store holds it, `state_source`, the set table `t_subscription__transitions`, `t_declarationchange`, the operator's identity indexes and the label's indexes among them; `of_actor`, `of_migration`, `of_file` and `of_file_ref` replaced by a note of where their jobs went, a file being the open question below; and the mentions of them in §3.1, §3.3, §5, §6's version 0 and §9's erasure steps 4 and 5 corrected or marked not built. Re-read by the same script: every documented built-in table matches the store's columns, and every table the store holds is documented.*
- [x] **Who deletes an erased file's bytes** (found 2026-10-15 reconciling `storage-schema.md` with the core; **decided 2026-10-17**, ADR-0211 decision 1: (b); *built the same day: `referenced(hashes)`, a read of every table's text for the hashes asked, in the core and the service, documented in `DESIGN.md` §5 and §10, `library-api.md`, `wire.md`, `integrate.md` and `storage-schema.md` §9; held by `a_file_is_referenced_until_its_last_copy_is_erased`, whose shared photo is held by nothing once both who held it are erased, and the service's `a_store_says_which_files_it_still_holds`; two mutants killed. Its cost measured 2026-10-17 on stores the release service made with `--create` and filled by imports of synthetic mirror objects, each a row and an import event holding a photo's hash and a 600-byte note, on a 12-core machine at a load of about 1, the file in the page cache: a 184 MiB store (52,000 objects, the two-year store's file being 182 MB) answered in 0.30 s at the median of three for a hash it holds nowhere, which reads it whole, and 0.31 s for 1,000 such hashes; a 550 MiB store (155,000 objects, the six-year store's 548 MB) in 0.89 s and 0.92 s. A scan costs a write nothing, so no reference index is kept; the profile's own stores were not read for it, the classifier refusing a scan of them over personal data.*). `DESIGN.md` §5 and §9, ADR-0017, ADR-0041 and ADR-0087 make deleting a file's bytes, once no unerased reference holds it, the engine's one byte-level operation; `integrate.md` gives storing, serving and deleting the bytes to the application; the core holds a file as its hash, size and type alone, with no `of_file` table and no access to the blob store. Probed: a `Person` whose personal `photo` is erased keeps nothing of it, the creation event's `photo` reading `null` and the erasure's payload `{"erased": ["photo"]}`, so nothing tells the application which bytes became unreferenced, and with identical files shared it cannot tell from its own records either. The bytes stay. Options: (a) the erasure's answer and event name the hashes it left unreferenced, for the application to delete, which keeps each such hash in the log for ever; (b) **a read answering which of the hashes given are still referenced by an unerased object**, which the application asks of its blob store's inventory after an erasure, or on a schedule, and deletes what nothing holds, keeping no erased hash anywhere; (c) as now, the application keeping its own references, which `integrate.md` would then have to say, with the sharing it cannot see. Recommended (b): it keeps erased values out of the log, sees sharing, and needs no access to the blob store. It is a new wire operation, and the documents above change with it. Before 1.0.0, as P (erasure reaches every copy) is at stake for any flow with a personal file.
- [x] **Author: question 16, the repository's settings** (ADR-0191 decision 7; **decided 2026-10-17**, ADR-0211 decision 2: (b) and (c), (a) when pushes go through pull requests). *Written 2026-10-17, for the author to apply: `.github/dependabot.yml`, for cargo, pip, npm and the workflows' actions, effective once pushed; `scripts/setup/github-settings.sh`, which creates the ruleset letting only administrators create, move or delete a `v*` tag, run with `gh` logged in as one. The repository held no ruleset when it was written (`gh api repos/rxdu/object_flow/rulesets` answered `[]`).* The fifth review found `main` taking a push whose `check-all` failed, `v*` tags unprotected and Dependabot off. Options: (a) require `check-all` and `audit` on `main`, which holds every push, the author's included, until CI passes, about 17 minutes; (b) a ruleset that lets only the author create or move a `v*` tag; (c) Dependabot for cargo, pip, npm and the workflows' actions, which opens pull requests on the repository. Recommended: (b) and (c) now, and (a) when pushes go through pull requests, since today it would hold each of the author's pushes for the run. Each is a setting on GitHub, through `gh api`, on the author's word. *Applied 2026-10-21 at the author's direction (ADR-0227 decision 1): the ruleset "release tags" made by `scripts/setup/github-settings.sh`, active, `v*` tags created, moved or deleted by administrators alone; (a) still waits on pushes going through pull requests.*

- [x] The minors (decision 6). *Done 2026-10-13: an identity's unshown characters (`test_an_identity_shows_what_it_is_alike`, through both backends); an import's kinds and named actors (`import::tests`, three cases); a filter's logic over booleans (`what_a_filter_a_key_and_a_store_are_told`); a dimension kept twice (`test_a_dimension_kept_twice_is_refused_alike`, failing with either backend's check gone); pages of events by bytes (`a_page_of_events_ends_at_its_bytes`); an unread answer (`an_answer_not_read_fails_within_the_idle_timeout`, its own wait bounded); a refused restore's message (`a_store_refused_beside_a_log_says_to_restore_the_copy_alone`); the guides on a stalled write's wait, a set-derived filter's cost, `time-sync.target` and a subscription's acts. A time computed past the calendar stays a time in a filter, as decision 8 now says: the reference runner holds a time untyped.*

Its hits, in `flow-review.md`'s closed appendix and `metric-scenarios.md`'s version 15 (whose expected notices now hold it), are the question below.*
- [x] **An optional value cannot be moved where there may be none** (found 2026-10-16 by the `absent` notice's first scan of the design documents). ADR-0201 decision 7 refuses an assignment whose value is absent, a copy as arithmetic is: probed through both backends with the tee, `other := count`, `held := peg` between optional references, and `held := if peg is not null then peg else held` are each refused `value_in_range` alike where both are absent. The language has no conditional step, so "move the peg if there is one" takes two transitions, guarded `peg is null` and `peg is not null`. It has two cases, not the one first recorded here: `flow-review.md`'s appendix, where `inventorize` assigns `procured_for := peg` and then clears `peg`, refusing a unit never pegged (the review is closed and its appendix kept as proposed, 2026-09-24, before decision 7, so it is noticed and left); and `metric-scenarios.md`'s version 15, which adopts that proposal as the scenarios' own (its decision 3). The scan of the design documents reported only the first, since the scenarios' versions are checked by `check-scenarios.py`, and the gate's replay found the second; version 15's expected notices now hold it. Its history does not reach the refusal: both units it inventorizes from version 15, R50 and R51, are pegged to D20 first. **For the author**, since it refines an accepted decision: a plain read of an optional attribute assigned to an optional attribute could clear the target where it is absent, as `clear` does, while arithmetic on an absent value stays refused (D655's `count := count + inputs.by`, where clearing would make a bump silently do nothing). Recommended: refine decision 7 so. The alternative keeps it and leaves the two-transition form, which the `absent` notice now points authors to. **Decided 2026-10-16** ("continue the loop with your recommendations"), ADR-0209 decision 5: as recommended, a plain read, a name or a path. *Built 2026-10-16 in both backends (`request.rs`'s assign, `flowrun.py`'s), held by `a_plain_read_of_an_absent_value_clears_an_optional_attribute` and its tee test, D655's tests unchanged; both checkers' `absent` notice skips such a read, its plants gaining a copy to a required attribute, noticed, and a move, quiet; `metric-scenarios.md` version 15's `absent` row removed again; eight mutants killed, three a backend and one a checker.*

An `int` divided by a `decimal` is refused at publish rather than run: §8.3 makes `/` yield its left operand's type, and an `int` quotient that truncates or rounds is a trap either way, so the refusal names `n * 1.0 / rate`, a decimal dividend; the engine had never run it, and the runner answered a decimal. The log now folds a line break in any line it says into a space, so every line is one stamped line, held by `a_deployments_edges_are_said`, which failed before the fix.*

D687. *Two of three done 2026-10-15, each probed first. `"by " + actor.id`: the core answered `501` and the runner joined the strings, a divergence; check 24's typers now type `actor`'s members as §8.1 does (`.id` identity, `.kind` actor kind, `.principal` string), so it is refused as a string and an identity. `count(d)`: both backends answered the metric read `501`; both checkers now refuse a metric's `count` of an expression (`flow-format.md` §6's table says so). Held by a check 24 case and a plant, four mutants killed. The third done the same day at run time, since a checker cannot know whether a request will name a principal: it was refused alike, `value_in_range`, as any assignment of an unknown value is, though no request need name one. A step or argument that is exactly `actor.principal` now skips its write where the request names none, as one that is exactly an unsupplied optional input does, in both backends (`request.rs` `unsupplied`, `flowrun.py`), `DESIGN.md` §5 and `declaration-syntax.md` §5.2 saying so; held by `a_principal_is_resolved_recorded_and_written_by_an_outcome` and a tee test, three mutants killed. Left as they are: `actor.principal` inside a larger expression is still unknown where none is named, and refused, as check 48 refuses an unsupplied input there at publish, which a check of its own could do for the principal; and the reference runner carries no principal at all, its `actor.principal` always absent (`flowrun.py` `member`), so a request naming one writes it in the core and skips it in the runner, untested by the tee, which names none.*

## Production readiness by ADR-0204

Decided by the author 2026-10-14 ("go with your recommendations on all six"), after an independent critique. Each finding of the audit is delivered as a check kept in the repository; the release is the author's decision on what remains, with ADR-0204 §8's floor.

**A. The records.**
- [x] ADR-0204 decided; ADR-0183 decision 1 superseded; PRD revision 25 revises N1. *Done 2026-10-14.*
- [x] `README.md` and `SECURITY.md` state ADR-0204 §10's limits. *Done 2026-10-15: `SECURITY.md` "The limits a 1.0.0 production accepts", each with its ADR, and `README.md` "Where it stands" naming them and pointing there.*

**B. The adversarial harness** (ADR-0204 §6, decision 9.2; `design/adversarial-harness.md`).
- [x] A crate driving the core in process from a seed, the shipped examples as fixtures, a transcript, a failing run reduced to its shortest failing prefix. *Done 2026-10-14: `core/crates/objectflow-harness`; a run held to its seed by a test.*

- [x] **A refusal an effect causes is not explained by its read set** (found 2026-10-16 building §1's read-set condition, at forty seeds of 300). ADR-0088 records the objects a transition's rules read; an effect reads too, `assign_to_lead`'s `assignee := space.lead` in `issues.yaml` reading the space, and an effect can refuse its request: arithmetic past its bound, `value_in_range`, or a call or a creation refused, `call_refused`. Such a refusal's attempt row names nothing its effect read, so its record does not explain it, and the harness's condition leaves an effect's stop out (`objectflow-harness` `redecided`). A satisfied event's effect is explained by what it wrote (ADR-0209). **For the author**, since it widens ADR-0088. (A) An attempt row's read set holds what its effects read before they stopped, besides its rules' reads, so every refusal replays over its record; a satisfied event's stays the rules' reads, its effect's outcome recorded as `wrote`. (B) Every event's read set holds its effects' reads too: one rule for both, at the cost of every event's read set. (C) ADR-0088 kept: a refusal by an effect is explained by its clause and its recorded request values alone. Recommended: (A), since a refusal is the decision PRD L2 most needs explained and its row is rare, while (B) grows every event for what `wrote` already explains. **Decided 2026-10-16** ("go ahead with your recommendations"), ADR-0210 decision 5: (A); *done the same day, an attempt row's read set holding, under `effect`, what the request's effects and cascades read before they stopped, held by `a_refusal_an_effect_causes_records_what_the_effect_read` and two mutants.*

ADR-0204 §3's V against the reference runner is the differential battery's (`apps/flow-studio/server/tests/test_battery.py`), every context but a filter, which the sandbox serves no `query` for.
